# GSQ224 bounded source admission and quality preparation

This checkpoint records completed retirement and provenance inspection, a preserved acquisition failure, and the frozen next quality experiment. It does not claim a fully authenticated GSQ shard or any new quality result. The progress snapshot is historical, not a live status endpoint.

All forty-eight generated files of the rejected minmax223 artifact were authenticated before any deletion. Their manifests, exact producer closure, budgets and results remain; the original installation is untouched. The task-local frozen copy of the existing retirement helper changes only the closed scope and makes its staging census hardlink aware. Its seven existing tests pass. The receipt records 42,781,961,312 retired file bytes, 40,157,184-byte physical peak, and 366,837,399,552 allocated staging bytes immediately after retirement. Later small protocol/source files add allocation.

The first Hub transfer stopped after a read timeout, the library's internal resume attempt and a DNS error. No complete payload was admitted. The raw failure is preserved. A subsequent read-only exact HTTP206 range check passed. A separately frozen acquisition uses four concurrent sixty-four-MiB ranges per batch, retained completed ranges, no per-range retries, a one-GB physical bound, normal pressure, three-GB headroom, a one-hour deadline, and the existing 430-GB allocated staging cap. Final admission still requires the pinned 37,623,740,192-byte whole-file SHA256. No secondary n-gram shard is requested.

The original base repository still has one non-README payload map across all three visible revisions. Alongside the GSQ card and GGUF base declaration this supports a same-published-checkpoint lineage inference, as in the existing VQ research. Exact producer and calibration provenance has not been independently replayed. The pinned GSQ source tree exposes no matching Flash Next conversion filename; this is bounded inspection, not proof that no such source exists anywhere.

The frozen GSQ224 quality protocol reuses the existing affine layer-streamed reference, original non-expert tensors and tokenizer contexts, prior three-bit control and proxy criteria. It first requires byte-identical three-bit conversion and the prior coding control's exact full-vocabulary hash. Then it evaluates only coding, tool-result and multilingual contexts, sixteen selected positions each. It passes the cheap screen only if no context loses more than two top choices relative to prior333, macro agreement loses no more than one sixteenth, and mean KL is at most 1.2 times prior333 plus .02. These unchanged thresholds are not task-quality certification.

The candidate changes only gate/up to GSQ Q2_0 with explicit BF16 scale rounding, preserving original four-bit down. Whole-shard authentication, header-prefix equality and retained-descriptor identity checks precede expert reads. The ten-GB physical bound and existing headroom/pressure/deadline checks remain. No new model payload or raw logits are exported. Pre-execution V1/V2 corrections remain preserved; neither executed. V3 binds the repaired acquisition path without changing inputs or criteria. The one-shot handoff waits for authenticated admission and downloader exit, then launches one quality screen. Failure prevents launch or further advancement. No model selection, installation, native full-model speed, completed-task quality or other-Mac claim follows.
